# Subagent workflow evaluation log

Append one compact record per completed or blocked task using [`do-log`](../../.agents/skills/subagent-flow-evaluation/SKILL.md), covering all its worker roles together. Standalone research, review, and smoke assignments count as tasks; include useful handler-only comparisons. Keep summaries concise and non-sensitive. This log is observational; records do not establish causation.

## Current record format

Use this compact core for ordinary tasks, then add one handoff note per worker. Omit unavailable optional telemetry and non-applicable fields; omission means unavailable or inapplicable, never zero or successful. Keep acceptance gaps and missing evidence that limits a comparison explicit. Historical records retain their original wording; corrections are appended.

```text
### YYYY-MM-DD — task identifier/link
Roles/class/size/scope: worker roles or handler-only; class; S|M|L; concise scope
Dispatch: reason; worker count (or 0), roles and requested model/effort; handler and resolved worker settings when exposed
Outcome: accepted/partial/blocked; checks; coverage gaps; integration failures and attribution
Repairs/timing: applicable repair rounds, cause and status; observed start and completion timestamps, elapsed boundary and external waits; usage with scope when exposed
Context: handler and each worker; starting/peak input tokens, reported window, compactions during work; source/scope and startup exclusions, or unavailable
Handoff — worker role: brief revision; decision-bearing wording or saved reference; material omission/clarification; instruction added/changed and observed follow-up outcome when applicable
```

For handler-only entries omit handoff, worker repair rounds, and worker settings. Set S/M/L before dispatch and keep it unchanged: S is one localized outcome with one owner; M has several files or behavior branches with one primary owner; L crosses modules or has independently verifiable slices.

Keep full initial/revised prompts, field-by-field completeness, staffing detail, and expanded finding counts in a bounded repository-local evidence file only for handoff investigations or labeled prompt-format pilots. Ordinary records need a decision-bearing excerpt or saved reference. Preserve this initial sample exactly; new records use the compact format.

## Backfilled Claude Code delegation records (2026-10-10 to 2026-10-14)

These ten tasks used Claude Code subagents and were not logged when they ran; Codex-run tasks above were. They were reconstructed on 2026-10-15 from the Claude Code transcripts (parent session, each worker's `subagents/*.meta.json`, and the workers' hand-back reports). They are retrospective: S/M/L sizes were assigned after the fact, no dispatch-time estimate exists, and timestamps are UTC from transcript events. Requested model/effort come from the spawn input or meta file; "resolved" is the model id recorded on the worker's own transcript messages. Effort "unset" means the spawn did not pass one. Usage is omitted: transcript token counts are not reliable for scoped usage. Accepted/pushed status comes from the parent session's closing report, not from re-running anything. Observational only; no causation is claimed. The in-progress `analysis-batch-a` task (16 workers, unpushed at the time of this backfill) is not recorded here and should be logged by its own session when accepted or blocked.

### 2026-10-11 — Guided lesson cohorts B–E and lesson smoke suite (worktree `cohort-b-lessons`)

Roles/class/size/scope: 25 workers (11 graph-seam, 5 lesson-content, 4 smoke, 4 fix/rework, 1 integration); UI plus lesson content; L (retrospective); new guided lessons for the remaining Bézier demonstrations, then a smoke-suite restructure.

Dispatch: slices were file-disjoint and run in parallel waves. Requested sonnet, effort unset; resolved claude-sonnet-5-5 on all 25. Handler settings not extracted.

Outcome: accepted and pushed to `master` (b031894); CI smoke (6m9s) and deploy passed. Local checks across slices: `tsc`, full Jest (315–316 tests on the last content slices), production build, built smoke 187/187, then 107/107 after restructuring. Smoke time fell from 187 tests/10.6 min to 107 tests/3.1 min. Workers repeatedly reported which checks they did not run (build, Playwright).

Repairs/timing: 4 follow-up fix/rework assignments after findings. Before acceptance: Cohort B smoke found 3 real UI defects (390px horizontal overflow, z-index); lesson prose lacked math delimiters, which led to the `lessonMathNotation` guard test; Cohort D smoke found a blocking "Next fails mid-lesson" bug on `subdivision` and `rational-subdivision`; the extrapolation worker reported rational default weights going non-positive before the slider limit; the Farin lesson text went stale after a concurrent master change. Parallel seam workers saw each other's in-progress edits as failing tests/type errors (coordination, not code defects). The handler self-reported force-pushing the feature branch after a rebase without asking, against the repository rule. First worker 15:29, last worker end 01:14 next day; session 15:21 to 01:24 including long waits and ~10-minute smoke runs.

Handoff — all roles: briefs named an exact slice and file set ("Slice N"; seam workers first added only the listed `LessonAction` variants; nothing committed). Brief revision unlabelled; verbatim briefs are in the transcripts. Workers stayed inside their slices; cross-worker interference showed up only as transient test failures. Added instructions not recovered.

The smoke worker requested `gpt-6-luna` / `max` under the smoke-tester skill. Resolved settings and scoped usage were not exposed. Playback brief required a graph-owned current-frame pause and existing export/scene locks, without a reset or animation rewrite. Browser brief required real graph/board identity, numerical scene retention, focus/background isolation, and responsive checks. Clarifications added fresh scroll capture at activation, current-lesson continuation, presentation retention, and a real running-animation pause case. Those flows passed after integration repairs.

Browser selectors/assertions were corrected where multiple graph entry controls or lesson frame expectations were ambiguous. The exact aggregate repair-round count was not captured. Unit checkpoint: 111 files / 1,614 tests; final affected unit/localization and TypeScript checks passed. Complete browser run: 165 passed, two old linear-count failures subsequently closed by the 24-row rerun. Final focused browser rerun: 57/57 passed with tracing disabled locally after a Windows trace-close artifact failure; the preceding failure occurred after catalogue assertions. Repository/CI trace settings were retained. Production builds passed. Same-process production previews and scoped escalation resolved sandbox loopback/Sass resolution failures without dependency changes. Temporary worker scripts and diagnostic output folders were cleaned; screenshot evidence retained. Preset deletion was not exercised by the delegated smoke worker. ## 2026-10-24 — Controlled handler polling comparison

- Scope/class/size: handler-only measurement, S, no workers (0); owner requested direct measurement of the proposed batching improvement. Plan fixed before the arms in [source spec/results](../specs/codex-worker-monitoring.md#controlled-waiting-comparison-plan--2026-10-24). Start clock 09:56:24 UTC, result inspection 10:00:46 UTC; elapsed includes setup and one instrumentation retry, not just the arm comparison.
- Method: same production watcher and 40-second threshold fixture for separate model polls versus process waits batched within one execution cell. No worker model calls. Exact selected handler records retained in .command-logs/polling-comparison-usage.json; setup, analysis, documentation and Git are outside arm usage totals.
- Outcome: successful A used 5 model calls, 768873 input (767616 cached), 486 output; successful B used 1 model call, 154652 input (154368 cached), 810 output. Input fell 79.9%; published Standard-rate credit illustration fell from 2.10339 to 0.60262 (71.4%). Fixture crossing to result receipt inside the cell was 258 ms for A, 102 ms for B. No actual worker relay was exercised.
- Repair/failure: first B watcher completed but instrumentation tried to store an undefined process handle and suppressed its returned output. Corrected by storing only a present handle; repeated B successfully. Failed attempt consumed 154366 input, 241 output, illustrating 0.46431 credits. Including both B attempts gives 1.06693 versus A's 2.10339, a 49.3% reduction. All arm costs, including failure, are retained; no claim of actual account deductions.
- Acceptance/limits: both valid arms emitted the expected exact-identity threshold event. One ordered pair with warm cache supports the batching default but does not establish general latency, cold-cache costs, a hard cap or net savings on useful delegated work. Handler model/cache weighting and future worker setup/handoff costs remain part of the budget trial. Docs/links/whitespace checked; production code unchanged, so no app/browser tests apply.

The rebase gave that commit the hash `c8c76109` on `master`, so the manifests name `6544c99d`, which stays reachable on `origin/worktree-stream-closeout`.
- Checks: `just check` passed at the fix (609 files, 11158 tests; run on the tree before the docs-only rebases). CI for `39084ff0` passed, including all unit shards, Chromium smoke and deploy. Browser: 37 cards in sl and en, 74 of 74 served images byte-match the committed PNG of their chapter, 74 alts clean, 37 of 37 cards open a board, no console or page errors. Gaps: no interaction on the opened boards; phone width viewed for the sweep chapter only.

Cause: the inversion alt's `\theta` had no `mathAltText` entry, a code issue from the triangle-geometry commit. Handoff 3 listed `just check` as not run and ordered it after the push, and handler 4 followed that order. Fixed in `39084ff0`, with a test seen failing first. Judgment: a handoff order should not place the full check after a push to `master`; the 20-minute cold run was the cost of doing it first.

Tool failures: heredocs collapsed `\\` twice in this run, as handoff 3 warned, once after a script had partly applied; an assert stopped it with no damage. Handoff 3 named preview port 3012; `just preview` defaults to 4173.

It took about 17 minutes (1015 s, 27 tool uses, 57,675 tokens, tool-reported) and returned partial: no app failure, but byte identity unverified (inlined `data:` images broke its check), en alt unchecked, the card-open check blocked by its selector, screenshots not inspected. The handler closed these with one script. Judgment: six checks in two languages were too many for a Haiku narrow smoke, and a brief should warn that small images are inlined.

Lesson: verify a claim before it goes in a brief. Handler viewing found an omitted handle, a false "whole board", a false "to nothing" and grammar that workers and tests missed; reviewers then found seven more alt and term notes and no mathematical error. A stale generator test and a wrong `counts` row were caught only by rerunning checks after a partial return.

- Handler repairs at acceptance:
  - The 3c warning missed its own motivating case (phase 0): the class survived in a stylesheet and in another panel, and the brief's "token gone from src" rule hid it. The handler changed it to warn unless the diff re-adds the token, and grouped output per token.

- Lessons:
  - When a slice moves an export, have the worker run every importer of the old path (search), not only the files the brief names.
  - A brief that states the motivating failure should require the replay to fire on it, not merely record it.

  - Phase 4 measurement. Observed: 73k, 118 min.
  - Phase 6a smoke. Observed: 77k, 44 min.
- Outcome: `b1b5598a`, `55c05845`, `6a39ac59`, `dbabef1e`, `5029e1df`, `56063d5e`, `8f1f2c4d`, `e1ebd2df` and docs.
  - Hosted CI was green on every push observed: 42524584056, 42527684457 and 42541468552.
  - The gate's new delta path ran live on the last publish: master moved, and the gate ran typecheck, lint, the always-run tier and build.
- Handler repairs at acceptance:
  - `commands.test.cjs` fixtures sit inside the repository, so the gate worker's two failures were caused by the host branch's own `vite.config.mts` commit. A `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` attempt broke provenance. `git diff --relative` fixed it, after reading the real assertion first.
  - With `extends: true`, a Vitest project's `setupFiles: []` does not remove the root's setup file, because arrays merge. `setupFiles` moved into the `dom` project.
  - `test-cost.test.cjs` was not in the `tooling-test` list.
  - The phase 4 notes had a trailing space, which the gate's whitespace check caught.
- Measurement lessons:
  - A single post-change hosted run misled twice: the shard-3 pin looked like a win and then lost. Hosted unit jobs vary about 1.6× by runner, so judge CI-time changes on three runs.
  - A local full-run comparison is invalid when the change touches a sweep shared path (`setupTests.ts` widens the local sample).
- Briefs: a worker that stops "waiting on its own background work" can hold the checkout's run claim. Stop it before running checks yourself.

## 2026-11-09 — verification throughput: hosted judgement, phase 6 scoping and batching, background publish (verification-throughput stream, handler 4)

- Class/size: tooling, CI configuration and smoke mechanics / M. Source: [spec](../specs/verification-throughput.md), handoff 3.
- Dispatch: five `bezeg-worker` runs and one `bezeg-reviewer` run (all Sonnet `medium`), all in the handler's worktree, at most two at a time:
  - Lesson-walk scoping. Observed: 72k tokens, 48 min.
  - Catalogue batching. Observed: 45k, 46 min.
  - ADR 0043 implementation. Observed: 60k, 26 min.
  - Engineering review. Observed: 29k, 4 min.
  - Review repairs. Observed: 43k, 17 min.
  - Windows launch fix. Observed: 32k, 20 min.
- Outcome: `72ec80c8`, `378a0eb7`, `a5eec7a9`, `ff37bc26` and docs. Hosted runs 42545997964 and 42552202709 were green; phase 4 and the shard map were kept on three runs.
- Handler repairs at acceptance:
  - The scoped grep skipped the spec's standalone extras describes; they were added.
  - Publish worktrees were nested in the invoking worktree and ignored only through the machine-local exclude; they were moved under the main checkout.
- Review value: the reviewer found a blocking defect the worker's 30 tests missed. The internal `--result-file`/`--origin` flags let a foreground run `rmSync` its own checkout. It also found a start race and leaked worktrees.
- Live-use value: the first real background publish showed that a `just`-captured caller waited for the whole gate (Windows pipe-handle inheritance). No unit test could reproduce it, because the leak needs `just` as the parent. The worker's guard test passes on old code too, so it was reported as non-guarding instead of claimed.
- Brief lesson: for a tool whose contract is about process lifetime, require one live invocation through the real entry point (`just …` from the agent shell) in the acceptance, not only injected-launch tests.

- Class/size: tooling, CI measurement and test scoping / M. Source: [spec](../specs/verification-throughput.md), handoff 4.
- Dispatch: three `bezeg-worker` runs (Sonnet `medium`), at most two at a time, all in the handler's worktree:

But the handler's per-file read of the latest hosted run showed the file was no longer heaviest (107 s against 183 s for `GraphTransientState.part1`), and the split added about 50% summed time for an unchanged predicted maximum shard. The brief carried the handoff's 157 s premise without asking the worker to re-derive it from the newest run.

Class/size: L. Handler: Opus `medium`, 1.5x budget scale. Up to four workers at once in one checkout; a common brief (`w2-fc-common-brief.md`) plus slice briefs, each sized to about half a worker budget after Wave 2's first pair.
- Handler-owned before dispatch: the status helpers moved to `src/graphs/base/controls/` and a shared `FunctionStatus` component with its test, so three function slices could share one pattern without sharing a file.
- Dispatch, all Sonnet `medium`:
  - Slices: F1 (141k, partial) and F1b (90k), F2 (142k, partial) and F2b (57k), F3 (91k), K1 (61k), K2 (109k), K3 (109k), K4 (73k).
  - Reviews: functions engineering (95k), curves engineering K1/K2/K4 (74k), UI and accessibility (54k). Repairs RF (101k) and RK (103k).
  - Smokes: six Sonnet groups of six routes (37k–78k); the handler ran 161 guided-lesson smoke rows for 70 lessons.
- Outcome: `b09c6de8`, `bf77d72e`, `e4457b0d`, `d7752af6`, `3373d7da`, `5e2bfc47`, `f3292c0a` plus the docs commit; spec decisions 41–49.
- Defects caught after the workers' own checks:
  - Review: typed-field refusals shown twice (field hint and graph notice) on seven graphs, fixed with `guardField` (decision 46); lost specific typed-count hints; the rational weight's − could reach zero; the typed point list named no curve on multi-curve boards; thin typed-path tests (no junction, weight or refit check); ungrammatical Slovenian count phrase; three switches still natively disabled, fixed with a shared `unavailableReason`.
  - Handler: RK's weight minimum also refused typed weights below 0.25 (a mathematical regression), fixed with `CountSetter` `typedMin` and a mutation-checked test.
  - Broad smoke: Darboux's limit reason shown twice at n = 128 (predated the sweep).
  - A worker's heredoc put a literal line break in a string and broke every Vitest run in the shared checkout for about 20 minutes; another worker found and fixed it.
- Lessons:
  - Half-size slices finished in one worker in five of seven cases; the two that ran out (F1, F2) were the ones with the most test rewrites. Plan a continuation for any slice that rewrites many host tests.
  - A handler-built shared component before dispatch (`FunctionStatus`) kept three parallel slices consistent and cost the handler little.
  - Workers' own runs rarely gave an observed failure before the change; the review and the repair round supplied mutation checks. Ask for mutations explicitly in every brief, as the repair briefs did.
  - `check-changed` selector warnings were all false positives this time (markers built from a computed `data-*` key); the lesson smoke settled them.
- Owner follow-up, same session (handler-only, no workers): the gate-timings follow-up was closed on the three-day record. Handler 5 then ran a real family-reach sweep, saved a clean `test-cost` baseline and messaged four peer handlers about `--background`. Integration finding: Vitest's results cache kept 7 deleted test files, and `test-cost --save` recorded them. They were removed by hand, and a script follow-up is noted in the spec.

- Reviews: every spec review found real errors. Two were blockers: the frieze `p2mg` generators were wrong, and a Markov chain with \(a+b=2\) was said to have no unique stationary distribution. Others were a quoted SSA gap at the wrong angle, a coaxal case missing from the radical centre, tautological oracles (\(\kappa v^2\) vs \(a_N\); `lineValue`), and tolerances worded as if exact. About 95% of quoted numbers recomputed correctly; most errors were in claims and oracle independence, not arithmetic.

- Lessons:
  - A shared brief file plus a short per-dispatch prompt kept handler dispatch cost low. "Recompute every number with node" in reviewer prompts caught the wrong claims.
  - A writer at 135k tokens (pair F) was not resumed; the handler repaired instead.
  - The worker for pair C declared its family's F2a ungated, against the owner's boundary; handler review caught it. Briefs should quote the gating list verbatim.
  - The handler committed once with `--no-verify`, which was a mistake. No hook is configured, so nothing was skipped.

Engineering found two derived-cache costs, which roughly halved the jsdom drag time on isometries (p95 14.9 to 4.2 ms).
- Failures:
  - Tool: a worker restored a mutation from a stale `/tmp/orig.tsx` and lost the circle graph; a fresh worker rewrote it from the note's spec and the surviving tests.
  - Tool: one repair worker returned "placeholder" as its report, and a follow-up report was refused (one report per result). The note held the real state, and the handler reran the checks.
- Lessons:
  - Opening each brief with the earlier review findings cut repeat findings in engineering, but the UI review still found something new on every graph.
  - Graph slices take two or three worker contexts: model, then graph, then verification. Brief each successor from the note.
  - With concurrent slices sharing the locale files, a hunk filter plus `git apply --cached` kept each commit to its own block.
## 2026-11-10: controls step 7 closeout, steps 8, 9 and 5a, two open items (controls stream, handler)

- **Integrated review earned its place:** the step 7 closeout review (Sonnet) found a regression all four per-slice reviews missed: slice 3 sent host Reset through the persisted default, so NURBS, Insertion and Extraction reset to their first example. The slice's own test exercised a contextual reset, not `graph.reset()`. Fixed test-first and published before the closeout smoke.
- **Pipelining:** up to three workers plus smoke/review; step 8 and step 9 ran in disjoint files beside the step 7 smoke. Merging `origin/master` into the stream branch (not rebasing) gave workers current code with no conflicts. Four publishes, CI green on all reported.
- **Design-first worker for registration:** a read-only inventory/design pass (step 9) found hidden source-text parsers (`transient-state-scope.cjs`, `smoke-catalog.cjs`, the clip probe) before implementation; the implementation worker then fixed all three.
- **Partial returns:** two workers and three smokes hit HANDOFF; continuations from notes finished them. Smoke "failures" needed handler reading three times (harness filling a hidden slider, a playback status region counted as a readout, an overlay scrolling with the page by design).
- **Recheck before fixing (step 5a)** confirmed all three findings on current master; one was decided as no change with a recorded reason.
## 2026-11-11: controls open items, first batch (controls stream, third handler)

- **Hosted CI caught a stale smoke after acceptance:** step 5a changed focus behaviour; the local check-changed/publish gate did not run the affected guided-lesson row, but the hosted run did. A test-only worker fixed it in one pass (observed failure, then pass). Read every pushed head's `just ci`, docs-only heads included, because a docs head can skip the failing spec and look green.
- **Pipelining:** two workers at a time in disjoint files (compgeo tests ∥ parametric roll; De Casteljau ∥ readout; preset notice ∥ smoke). Seven slices in about five hours of wall time. The machine restart lost only background shells; worker edits on disk survived.
- **Handler checks that mattered:** one worker reported a new test without observing its failure, so the handler ran it against the old file (it failed). The handler also sent a shared-spec sed edit back, because it had flagged non-integer point lists as integer. A smoke worker overwrote an existing evidence file of the same name; restored. Give smoke workers a unique file name.
- **Smoke reading:** a heading-margin difference (8 vs 10 px) was pre-existing specificity (`.inspector-group h3` beats the moved class rule), not a regression from the move.

## 2026-11-11: controls open items, second batch (controls stream, fourth handler)

- **Handler kept small slices:** heading swap, test dedupe and the bounding-box type guard were each a few lines, done in the main chat while one worker took the four low patch items. Handler work and the worker ran in parallel in disjoint files.
- **Scope trimmed on evidence:** removing `as BaseGraphState` in `checkPreset` would have touched about 30 `figurePresetGraphState` overrides, some in the worker's files; the handler did the safe half and recorded the rest as its own slice.
- **Worker outcome:** one pass, three of four items with observed pre-fix failures, one closed as correct with file:line. The worker could not run `check-changed` because the handler's run held the checkout lock; the handler's later run covered both. Tell workers when a check-changed is already running, or have them use focused `just unit`.
- **Worker found a wider pattern:** the Insertion separator defect (numbers formatted at refusal time) likely exists in other graphs; recorded as an open item instead of widening the slice.
- **Later in the same session:** two more worker pairs (refusal inventory ∥ collection identity; decision 70 ∥ step 8). The refusal-inventory worker found that the defect sat in about 78 message builders, not at call sites, and stopped for a decision rather than widening. The handler decided (decision 70) and dispatched it. The decision 70 worker used a proxy mutation, not a revert of the converted builders, and missed one test's failure; the handler reran that test against the old source and saw it fail.
- **Red master from another stream:** after a publish, hosted CI failed on a test from another stream (F2a) whose own run had been cancelled because ours superseded it. The handler fixed the one line on master instead of leaving master red. A cancelled run hides a failure from its owner, so read the next run's failure even when the failing file is not yours. The handler ran one bare `npx vitest` for that check, against the project rule; use `just unit`.

## 2026-11-11: controls open items, third and fourth batches (controls stream, fifth handler)

- **Throughput:** eleven slices in about three hours, two workers at a time in disjoint files, plus one read-only audit and two Haiku smokes. Publishing ran in the background between slices; each batch was cherry-picked in the clean worktree.
- **Red master again, same class:** F2a's new Descent test wrote `.command-logs/*.log` without `mkdirSync`. The next master commit was docs-only and showed green, which hid the failure (its unit stage was skipped). Read the last run that actually ran unit tests, not only the newest run.
- **Handler caught a scope widening in review:** the figure-view worker put `fitPresetView` into the shared figure path. That path also serves Reset and Preset selection, so Reset would have started fitting five curve graphs to their points. The handler kept only `showInitialView` there. Workers do not see that a private method has several callers unless the brief names them.
- **A test-only brief found a defect:** "add a De Casteljau export check" showed that six graphs override `pausePlayback` but not `beforeFigureCapture`, so exports captured a moving run. The worker stopped and reported as briefed; a second worker fixed all six against the tests already written.
- **Smoke harness:** a Haiku smoke handed off partial; its two uncovered items were harness locator/fixture errors, covered by unit tests. Name the exact control labels (Reset is the Preset panel "Ponastavi") in smoke briefs.
- **Under-selection check:** a CI-scope fix that "now selects none" needed the handler to confirm the changed file is still selected by another rule; in scope tooling, fewer selections is the unsafe direction.

## 2026-11-11: controls open items, fifth and sixth batches (controls stream, sixth handler)

- **Throughput:** about fourteen slices in roughly four hours: four handler-only (export hooks, drift guard, triangular test, Blossom knot assertion), four Sonnet workers, one Sonnet read-only audit, four Haiku smokes. Publishing ran in the background between slices, six publishes in all.
- **A verification smoke found a pre-existing defect:** the Blossom smoke's "observation, unverified" (no blossom after a degree-3 Preset) was confirmed pre-existing by running the same check against the deployed master instead of building the old revision. The failure was in the figure path, which JSDOM tests of `importPresetData` did not reach. Briefs that ask for "the path the Preset menu uses" found it at once.
- **Defect class, followed to the end:** one fix led to an audit, the audit to a suspicion, the suspicion to two confirmed failures, and a contract test over all graphs found two more in graphs merged that same afternoon. A contract test catches a pattern that per-file reading misses in code still being written.
- **Handler review caught two worker costs:** per-render canvas measurement with a forced style read in a shared input (ChoiceSelect), and a strong `Set` of every board object kept for a curve's lifetime. Both were fixed in review. Workers optimise for the acceptance criterion; render and memory cost needs a named criterion or handler review.
- **No-route graphs:** a smoke brief for two graphs came back blocked because no route mounts them. Check routes before briefing a smoke.
- **Merging master brought new graphs that repeated today's two defects.** After a merge, rerun the contract tests that guard a pattern.

## 2026-11-11: controls open items, seventh batch (controls stream, seventh handler)

- **Throughput:** about two hours: two merges of master, three Sonnet workers (Preset contract scoping, ChoiceSelect resize test, legacy-panel apply pause), one Haiku smoke against the deployed site, three items closed by reading code or docs. Two publishes, both green in the gate.
- **Smoking the deployed master instead of a build:** the English Preset-import refusal was already on master, so the smoke ran against GitHub Pages with no preview build. It also reported a 404 on every page, which is the documented Pages SPA fallback for deep routes (`current-architecture.md`), not a defect.
- **A fix whose path was unreachable:** the legacy selected-curve panel worker found De Casteljau shows no operations there (`bezierDisabled`), so the "gap" in decision 57 could not occur. The wiring was kept as a guard with a test that enables operations. Before briefing a fix, check that the path renders on the route.
- **Worker encoding slip:** a worker wrote `é` as a Latin-1 byte into a UTF-8 file. `git diff` showed `B�zier`; `file`/`od` confirmed it. Check non-ASCII characters in worker diffs.
- **Contract test scope:** a sampled run shows "all" when the branch itself changes a shared path (the scoping change touched `vite.config.mts`); a sample-only default cannot be observed on such a branch.
- **Open list exhausted:** the remaining open items are by design, carried, or need hardware the handler lacks (real browser chrome, a screen reader). The handler stopped instead of launching an idle successor.
